### EngagementUIModule

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI` · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/EngagementUIModule.cs:17` · Level 11 · class (sealed)

- **What it is**: the Engagement module's UI descriptor. It implements [`IUIModule`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#iuimodule) to contribute six navigation items, one invisible layout component, and its own assembly for Blazor component discovery.
- **Depends on**: [`IUIModule`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#iuimodule), [`NavItem`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#navitem) and [`NavSection`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#navsection) from `MMCA.Common.UI.Common`, [`RoleNames`](group-08-auth.md#rolenames) from `MMCA.Common.Shared.Auth` (`EngagementUIModule.cs:3`), [`EngagementRoutePaths`](#engagementroutepaths), [`LiveEventListener`](#liveeventlistener), MudBlazor `Icons`, and `System.Reflection`.
- **Concept, the pluggable UI module descriptor.** `[Rubric §18, UI Architecture]` assesses how a modular monolith composes its front end without the host hard-coding each module's menu: every module ships an `IUIModule`, and the shared shell merges their nav items, layout components and assemblies. `[Rubric §25, Navigation & IA]`: the menu is declarative data here, not imperative wiring. `[Rubric §11, Security]`: three of the six items carry `RequiredRole: RoleNames.Organizer` (`:25`, `:26`, `:28`) so the shared `NavMenu` hides them from attendees; this is menu trimming, and the real enforcement remains the `[Authorize(Roles = "Organizer")]` attribute on each page. `[Rubric §27, i18n]`: per the [ADR-027](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/027-multi-locale-i18n.html) comment (`:19-20`), each `Title` is a resource **key**, resolved by `NavMenu` against the co-located `EngagementUIModule.resx` pair at render time through the `TitleResource: typeof(EngagementUIModule)` pointer, so the labels localize instead of being hard-coded English.
- **Walkthrough**
  - `NavItems` (`:21-29`) is a collection expression of six entries, each pairing a resource key, a centralized route and an icon: `Nav.HappeningNow` -> `HappeningNow` (`:23`), `Nav.MyBadge` -> `MyBadge` (`:24`), `Nav.CheckIn` -> `CheckInScan` (`:25`, Organizer), `Nav.Attendance` -> `OrganizerAttendance` (`:26`, Organizer), `Nav.MyPoints` -> `MyPoints` (`:27`), and `Nav.PointsOverview` -> `OrganizerPointsOverview` (`:28`, Organizer). `Nav.MyPoints` is the only one that sets `Section: NavSection.User`, which places it in the user section beside the profile entry rather than in the main menu.
  - `LayoutComponentTypes` (`:31`): `[typeof(LiveEventListener)]`, an invisible component the shell renders on every page; the class summary says it joins the event live channel while the event is live (`:11-15`).
  - `Assembly` (`:33`): `typeof(EngagementUIModule).Assembly`, so Blazor's router and component discovery can find this module's routable pages.
- **Why it's built this way**: exposing nav, layout components and the assembly as read-only properties lets the host treat every module identically, and keeping the descriptor beside the routes it points at means adding a page is a two-line change in one project. Note what is deliberately absent: the two self-service QR routes (`EngagementRoutePaths.cs:29-32`) contribute no nav item because they are reachable only by scanning a printed code.
- **Where it's used**: registered as a singleton `IUIModule` by [`DependencyInjection`](#dependencyinjection)'s `AddEngagementUI` (`MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:69`); consumed by the shared shell's `NavMenu` and router.

### DependencyInjection

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI` · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:15` · Level 12 · class (static)

- **What it is**: the Engagement UI composition root. One static class exposing an `AddEngagementUI()` extension on `IServiceCollection` that registers every Engagement UI service (bookmarks, reminders, feedback, check-in, points, lookups, the live layer, notification scoping) plus the [`EngagementUIModule`](#engagementuimodule) descriptor.
- **Depends on**: `IServiceCollection` and `Microsoft.Extensions.DependencyInjection.Extensions` (for `TryAddSingleton`), the Engagement UI service interfaces and implementations, [`ISessionBookmarkUIService`](#isessionbookmarkuiservice) and [`ISessionLiveUIService`](group-23-engagement-live-layer.md#isessionliveuiservice) from `.Shared`, [`IUIModule`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#iuimodule) and [`INotificationScopeProvider`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#inotificationscopeprovider) from the Common UI framework.
- **Concept, `extension(IServiceCollection)` registration.** `[Rubric §5, Vertical Slice]` and `[Rubric §1, SOLID]` assess whether a slice owns its own wiring: this is the single place the Engagement UI declares its services, so a host calls `AddEngagementUI()` and stays ignorant of the internals. The C# preview `extension(IServiceCollection services)` block (`DependencyInjection.cs:17`) is the DI-registration idiom used across the codebase (see the primer on [`extension(T)` members](00-primer.md#c-extensiont-types-read-this-once)), which is what lets `AddEngagementUI` read as an instance method on the collection.
- **Walkthrough**: `AddEngagementUI()` (`:23`) registers in labelled groups, all scoped (per circuit) unless noted.
  - Bookmarks (`:26-27`): [`IBookmarkUIService`](#ibookmarkuiservice) to [`BookmarkService`](#bookmarkservice), [`ISessionBookmarkUIService`](#isessionbookmarkuiservice) to [`SessionBookmarkUIService`](#sessionbookmarkuiservice).
  - [`SessionReminderCoordinator`](#sessionremindercoordinator) (`:31`), registered as a concrete type. The comment ([ADR-042](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/042-device-capability-abstraction.html) Wave 2, `:29-30`) records that it keeps local notifications in sync with bookmarks and is a pure no-op on hosts whose local-notification service is unsupported.
  - Feedback (`:34-36`): [`IQuestionLookupService`](#iquestionlookupservice) to [`QuestionLookupService`](#questionlookupservice), [`IEventFeedbackUIService`](#ieventfeedbackuiservice) to [`EventFeedbackService`](#eventfeedbackservice), [`ISessionFeedbackUIService`](#isessionfeedbackuiservice) to [`SessionFeedbackService`](#sessionfeedbackservice).
  - Badge check-in (`:40`): [`ICheckInUIService`](#icheckinuiservice) to [`CheckInService`](#checkinservice), the one service behind the attendee badge, both organizer write paths and the attendance rollup.
  - Points (`:44`): [`IPointsUIService`](#ipointsuiservice) to [`PointsService`](#pointsservice).
  - Cross-module lookups (`:47-48`): [`ISessionLookupService`](group-23-engagement-live-layer.md#isessionlookupservice) to [`SessionLookupService`](group-23-engagement-live-layer.md#sessionlookupservice) and [`IAttendeeLookupService`](#iattendeelookupservice) to [`AttendeeLookupService`](#attendeelookupservice), the two contracts the check-in surfaces use to name sessions and people that other services own.
  - Live layer (`:51-53`): [`ILivePollUIService`](group-23-engagement-live-layer.md#ilivepolluiservice), [`ILiveEventUIService`](group-23-engagement-live-layer.md#iliveeventuiservice), [`INowNextService`](#inownextservice); Wave 2 adds [`ISessionQuestionUIService`](group-23-engagement-live-layer.md#isessionquestionuiservice) and [`ISessionLiveUIService`](group-23-engagement-live-layer.md#isessionliveuiservice) (`:57-58`).
  - Notification scoping (`:65-66`): `services.TryAddSingleton(TimeProvider.System)` then a plain `AddScoped<INotificationScopeProvider, CurrentEventNotificationScopeProvider>()`. Both lines carry load-bearing comments (`:60-64`): the plain `AddScoped` deliberately **overrides** the null provider that `AddNotificationUI` registers with `TryAddScoped`, so the two calls stay order-independent; and `TimeProvider` is registered here because it drives the provider's cache expiry and no UI head registers it (the heads never call `AddInfrastructure`, which is where the framework's own `TryAddSingleton` lives).
  - Module descriptor (`:69`): `AddSingleton<IUIModule, EngagementUIModule>()`, a singleton because the descriptor is immutable metadata, while everything else is scoped since it holds per-circuit state.
  - Returns `services` (`:71`) for fluent chaining.
- **Why it's built this way**: one extension method per module UI keeps registration cohesive and lets a host compose modules by convention. The `TryAdd` versus `Add` choices are the part worth reading closely: `TryAdd` where this module is a *fallback* provider (`TimeProvider`), plain `Add` where it must *win* over a framework default (the notification scope provider). `[Rubric §10, Cross-Cutting Concerns]`: getting that pair wrong is exactly how a bell would silently stop scoping to the current event.
- **Where it's used**: called by the Blazor UI host's startup when the Engagement module is enabled, alongside the other modules' `AddXUI()` calls.

### AnswerState

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Pages.Feedback` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Pages/Feedback/EventFeedback.razor.cs:271` · Level 0 · class (private sealed, nested)

- **What it is**: a tiny mutable holder for one question's in-progress answer, used as the two-way binding target on the feedback forms. The same private nested class is declared independently inside both [`EventFeedback`](#eventfeedback) (`EventFeedback.razor.cs:271`) and [`SessionFeedback`](#sessionfeedback) (`SessionFeedback.razor.cs:304`); it is not a shared DTO.
- **Depends on**: nothing first-party (two BCL primitives only).
- **Concept introduced, the mutable form-state holder for two-way binding.** `[Rubric §19, State Management]` assesses where transient UI state lives and who owns it; here each page keeps the working answer set in a `Dictionary<QuestionIdentifierType, AnswerState>` (`EventFeedback.razor.cs:51`) rather than in the DTO it will eventually submit, so an in-progress edit never has to round-trip through a server contract. `[Rubric §24, Forms, Validation & UX Safety]` assesses form-input handling: MudBlazor's `MudRating` and `MudTextField` bind with `@bind-SelectedValue` / `@bind-Value` (`EventFeedback.razor:47-49`, `EventFeedback.razor:52`, `EventFeedback.razor:60`), which requires a settable property, so a `record` with `init`-only members could not serve as the bind target. Making the holder a small mutable class is the deliberate consequence of that binding requirement, not an oversight of the workspace's `required`/`init` immutability default.
- **Walkthrough**: two auto-properties (`EventFeedback.razor.cs:273-274`): `TextValue` (`string?`) backs open-text and email questions; `RatingValue` (`int`, default `0`) backs star-rating questions. The page's `GetAnswerValue` (`EventFeedback.razor.cs:258`) reads whichever member matches the question type, and treats `RatingValue > 0` as the only answered state (`EventFeedback.razor.cs:265-267`), so an untouched rating serializes to `null` and is skipped on submit. Clearing a saved answer resets both members in place (`EventFeedback.razor.cs:210-211`) rather than replacing the holder, which keeps the binding target the grid of MudBlazor inputs already captured.
- **Why it's built this way**: the class is `private sealed` and duplicated per page on purpose. Its only job is to carry transient form state before submission, so elevating it into a shared, non-private type would add coupling across two pages for no behavioral gain. The two copies are structurally identical (`SessionFeedback.razor.cs:306-307` matches `EventFeedback.razor.cs:273-274`).
- **Where it's used**: seeded one-per-question in `OnInitializedAsync` (`EventFeedback.razor.cs:87`, `SessionFeedback.razor.cs:94`), populated by `PreFillExistingAnswers` (`EventFeedback.razor.cs:128-132`), read by `GetAnswerValue` during `SubmitFeedbackAsync`, and reset by `DeleteAnswerAsync` on both feedback pages.

### CheckInState

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Pages.Rooms` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Pages/Rooms/RoomCheckIn.razor.cs:96` · Level 0 · enum (protected, nested)

- **What it is**: the five outcomes one scan of a printed room QR can have, as seen by the attendee. It is nested inside [`RoomCheckIn`](#roomcheckin) and is the single field its markup switches on.
- **Depends on**: nothing (a bare `enum`). Its values are produced from [`CheckInErrorCodes`](#checkinerrorcodes) by the page's mapping switch.
- **Concept introduced, the page-owned outcome enum.** `[Rubric §19, State Management]` assesses whether a component's visible states are enumerated or reconstructed ad hoc from a scatter of booleans. This page has exactly one state field, so "loading", "recorded", "nothing scheduled here", "event not published" and "unavailable" are mutually exclusive by construction, and the markup is a flat chain of comparisons (`RoomCheckIn.razor:12`, `:16`, `:41`, `:47`, `:51`) with no combination to reason about. `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` is the other half of the story: two of the refusals arrive as the same HTTP 404, so the state cannot be picked from a status code; the server's stable error code carried back on [`SelfCheckInOutcome<TResult>`](#selfcheckinoutcometresult) is what makes the distinction possible.
- **Walkthrough**: five members in the order the attendee meets them. `Loading` (`RoomCheckIn.razor.cs:99`) is the initial value of `State` (`RoomCheckIn.razor.cs:33`) and covers the in-flight post. `Recorded` (`:102`) covers both a fresh check-in and a re-confirmed earlier one; the distinction is `RoomCheckInResultDTO.AlreadyCheckedIn`, not a separate state. `NoCurrentSession` (`:105`) means nothing is scheduled in that room right now, and, per the member comment, an unknown room reads the same way. `NotPublished` (`:108`) means the owning event is not published yet. `Unavailable` (`:111`) is the catch-all for a disabled feature or an unreachable service.
- **Why it's built this way**: collapsing "unknown room" into `NoCurrentSession` and the feature gate into `Unavailable` is a deliberate disclosure choice: a printed code that produces a distinguishable answer for a room that does not exist would confirm which room ids do exist. See [ADR-072](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/072-qr-badge-check-in-and-points.html) for the surrounding scan-surface decision.
- **Where it's used**: only inside [`RoomCheckIn`](#roomcheckin) (`RoomCheckIn.razor.cs:33`, `:59`, `:69-74`, `:82`) and its markup. `protected` rather than `private` so the bUnit component tests can assert the rendered outcome (see [`RoomCheckInTests`](group-27-testing-infrastructure.md#roomcheckintests)).

---

### VisitState

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Pages.Sponsors` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Pages/Sponsors/SponsorVisit.razor.cs:96` · Level 0 · enum (protected, nested)

- **What it is**: the four outcomes one scan of a printed sponsor-booth QR can have. The sibling of [`CheckInState`](#checkinstate), nested inside [`SponsorVisit`](#sponsorvisit), with one fewer member because a sponsor booth has no "what is running here right now" question to answer.
- **Depends on**: nothing (a bare `enum`); its values are chosen from [`CheckInErrorCodes`](#checkinerrorcodes).
- **Concept**: the page-owned outcome enum is taught on [`CheckInState`](#checkinstate); the same reasoning applies unchanged here.
- **Walkthrough**: `Loading` (`SponsorVisit.razor.cs:99`), the initial value of `State` (`:34`); `Recorded` (`:102`), which again covers both a first visit and a repeat, split in the markup by `SponsorVisitResultDTO.AlreadyVisited` (`SponsorVisit.razor:19`); `NotPublished` (`:105`); and `Unavailable` (`:108`). The mapping is a ternary rather than a switch because only one error code is distinguished (`SponsorVisit.razor.cs:72-74`): `CheckInErrorCodes.EventNotPublished` selects `NotPublished`, and everything else, including a null code, falls to `Unavailable`.
- **Why it's built this way**: the member comment on `Unavailable` (`SponsorVisit.razor.cs:107`) folds "feature off", "unknown sponsor" and "service unreachable" into one member, and the markup comment (`SponsorVisit.razor:56-57`) states the reason: the three are the same sentence to an attendee holding a phone, and naming them apart would only confirm which sponsor codes exist. That makes the collapse a `[Rubric §26, Front-End Security]` decision (no existence disclosure through error differentiation) as much as a copy decision.
- **Where it's used**: only inside [`SponsorVisit`](#sponsorvisit) (`SponsorVisit.razor.cs:34`, `:62`, `:72-74`, `:82`) and its markup; `protected` so [`SponsorVisitTests`](group-27-testing-infrastructure.md#sponsorvisittests) can drive it.

### RoomCheckIn

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Pages.Rooms` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Pages/Rooms/RoomCheckIn.razor.cs:19` · Level 5 · class (partial)

- **What it is**: the code-behind for the room QR landing page (`@page "/engage/rooms/{RoomId:int}"`, `RoomCheckIn.razor:1`). An attendee scans the code printed beside a room door, signs in if needed, lands here, and the page records the check-in itself. The scan never names a session: the server decides which session that room is hosting at this instant.
- **Depends on**: [`ICheckInUIService`](#icheckinuiservice) (the post), [`SelfCheckInOutcome<TResult>`](#selfcheckinoutcometresult) (the return shape), [`RoomCheckInResultDTO`](#roomcheckinresultdto) (the payload), [`CheckInErrorCodes`](#checkinerrorcodes) (the refusal vocabulary), and its own nested [`CheckInState`](#checkinstate). Externals: Blazor `ComponentBase` (`RendererInfo`, `[Parameter]`), MudBlazor `ISnackbar` / `BreadcrumbItem` / `MudAlert`, `System.Globalization`, and an injected `IStringLocalizer<RoomCheckIn>` named `L` from the markup (`RoomCheckIn.razor:5`).
- **Concept introduced, the write-on-arrival landing page.** `[Rubric §18, UI Architecture]` assesses component/behavior separation and render-mode discipline. This page inverts the usual "render, then let the user press something" shape: the arrival *is* the interaction, so the round trip runs from `OnInitializedAsync`. That makes the Blazor render-mode question load-bearing, because a prerendered pass and the interactive pass both run `OnInitializedAsync`, and the whole point of the page is a write. The two guards at `RoomCheckIn.razor.cs:46-51` (`!RendererInfo.IsInteractive` and a `_posted` latch) are what keep one arrival to one post. `[Rubric §19, State Management]` covers the single-field state model taught on [`CheckInState`](#checkinstate). `[Rubric §11, Security]` applies through `@attribute [Authorize]` (`RoomCheckIn.razor:2`): the page identifies the attendee from the authenticated session, so a scanned code carries no identity of its own.
- **Walkthrough** (teaching order)
  - `[Parameter] RoomIdentifierType RoomId` (`RoomCheckIn.razor.cs:22`) is bound from the `{RoomId:int}` route constraint, so an unparseable code never reaches the component. The injected [`ICheckInUIService`](#icheckinuiservice) and `ISnackbar` follow (`:24-25`), then a `CancellationTokenSource` (`:27`), the breadcrumb list (`:29`) and the `_posted` latch (`:30`).
  - `State` (`RoomCheckIn.razor.cs:33`) starts at `CheckInState.Loading` and is `protected` with a private setter; `Result` (`:36`) holds the [`RoomCheckInResultDTO`](#roomcheckinresultdto) and is documented as present only in the `Recorded` state.
  - `OnInitializedAsync` (`:38`) builds the two-item breadcrumb trail first (`:40-44`) so even the non-interactive prerender has a complete chrome, then returns early if the render is not interactive or the post already ran (`:46-49`). Only after that does it set `_posted = true` (`:51`) and await `CheckInService.RecordRoomCheckInAsync(RoomId, _cts.Token)` (`:55`).
  - The success branch (`:57-67`) assigns `Result`, moves to `CheckInState.Recorded`, and raises a success snackbar **only** when `Result?.AlreadyCheckedIn == false` (`:61-63`): a rescan is a normal thing to do, so it renders as information (`RoomCheckIn.razor:22-24`) rather than as a second celebration.
  - The refusal branch (`:69-74`) is a `switch` expression over `outcome.ErrorCode`: `CheckInErrorCodes.NoCurrentSession` becomes `NoCurrentSession`, `CheckInErrorCodes.EventNotPublished` becomes `NotPublished`, and every other code (including `null`) falls through the discard arm to `Unavailable`. This is the client-side consumer of the error-code contract that [`SelfCheckInOutcome<TResult>`](#selfcheckinoutcometresult) exists to carry.
  - `catch (OperationCanceledException)` (`:76-79`) is silent (expected on disposal); any other exception lands on `Unavailable` (`:80-83`), so a transport fault and a disabled feature look identical to the attendee.
  - `FormatCheckedInOn` (`:92-93`) renders the recorded instant with `ToLocalTime()` and the `"g"` format under `CultureInfo.CurrentCulture`. The XML comment (`:86-89`) gives the reason: the attendee is comparing the timestamp against the clock on the wall beside them, so a UTC reading would be the one thing on the page they cannot check. `[Rubric §27, Internationalization]` covers both halves of that, the culture-aware format and the fact that every visible string is an `L["..."]` resource lookup ([ADR-027](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/027-multi-locale-i18n.html)).
  - `Dispose(bool)` / `Dispose()` (`:116-136`) implement the standard disposable pattern with a `_disposed` latch, cancelling and disposing `_cts` so an in-flight post stops when the attendee navigates away.
- **Why it's built this way**: the class comment (`RoomCheckIn.razor.cs:9-18`) states the two constraints. The write runs on the interactive render only, and only once per page instance: a prerender must not spend a round trip, and a browser back button must not read as a second arrival. The server is idempotent per session regardless (a repeat returns `AlreadyCheckedIn`), so the latch is about not making a pointless call rather than about correctness. Resolving the session server-side from the room plus the configured grace window, instead of encoding it in the QR, means the printed code never expires when the schedule moves ([ADR-072](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/072-qr-badge-check-in-and-points.html)). The page complements organizer badge scanning at the door rather than replacing it: one session yields one check-in by either path, which is exactly what the "already checked in" branch says (`RoomCheckIn.razor:20-21`).
- **Where it's used**: the target of the room QR rendered on the Conference room detail page (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.UI/Pages/Room/RoomDetail.razor:56`, a `QrCodeButton` over [`ConferenceRoutePaths`](group-21-conference-ui.md#conferenceroutepaths)`.RoomCheckInLink`, `ConferenceRoutePaths.cs:56`) per [ADR-071](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/071-barcode-scanning-and-qr-display.html). The post lands on [`CheckInsController`](#checkinscontroller)'s room-visits endpoint (`CheckInsController.cs:139`, feature-gated on `EngagementFeatures.RoomCheckIn`) and is handled by [`RecordRoomCheckInHandler`](#recordroomcheckinhandler). `[Rubric §28, Front-End Testing]`: covered by [`RoomCheckInTests`](group-27-testing-infrastructure.md#roomcheckintests) (bUnit).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the outcome copy, the success card and the points hint live in `RoomCheckIn.razor` and the resource files, not in this code-behind. Whether a given deployment has `EngagementFeatures.RoomCheckIn` on is configuration, not source.

### SponsorVisit

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Pages.Sponsors` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Pages/Sponsors/SponsorVisit.razor.cs:20` · Level 5 · class (partial)

- **What it is**: the code-behind for the sponsor-booth QR landing page (`@page "/engage/sponsors/{SponsorId:int}"`, `SponsorVisit.razor:1`). The attendee scans the booth's printed code with the phone camera, signs in if needed, and lands here; the page records the visit itself, so the whole interaction is one scan with nothing to press.
- **Depends on**: [`ICheckInUIService`](#icheckinuiservice), [`SelfCheckInOutcome<TResult>`](#selfcheckinoutcometresult), [`SponsorVisitResultDTO`](#sponsorvisitresultdto), [`CheckInErrorCodes`](#checkinerrorcodes), its nested [`VisitState`](#visitstate), and (from the markup) [`ConferenceRoutePaths`](group-21-conference-ui.md#conferenceroutepaths) for the follow-on "all sponsors" link. Externals as on [`RoomCheckIn`](#roomcheckin): Blazor `RendererInfo` / `[Parameter]`, MudBlazor `ISnackbar` and friends, `System.Globalization`, and an injected `IStringLocalizer<SponsorVisit>` (`SponsorVisit.razor:6`).
- **Concept**: the write-on-arrival landing page is taught on [`RoomCheckIn`](#roomcheckin); this page is the same shape with a simpler refusal map. The one difference worth naming is what happens *after* success: `[Rubric §25, Navigation & Information Architecture]` assesses whether a dead-end page gives the reader somewhere to go, and this one ends with a filled "all sponsors" button back into the public sponsor list (`SponsorVisit.razor:42-48`), which the room page has no equivalent of because a room scan is followed by sitting down.
- **Walkthrough of what differs from [`RoomCheckIn`](#roomcheckin)**
  - `[Parameter] SponsorIdentifierType SponsorId` (`SponsorVisit.razor.cs:23`) instead of a room id; `State` defaults to `VisitState.Loading` (`:34`) and `Visit` holds a [`SponsorVisitResultDTO`](#sponsorvisitresultdto) (`:37`).
  - `OnInitializedAsync` (`:39`) carries an inline comment naming the pattern it follows (`:47-48`): the visit is a write, so it stays off the prerender pass, and the interactive instance is the one that owns the round trip (the [`ADCHome`](group-21-conference-ui.md#adchome) pattern). The same `!RendererInfo.IsInteractive || _posted` guard follows (`:49-52`), then `CheckInService.RecordSponsorVisitAsync(SponsorId, _cts.Token)` (`:58`).
  - Success (`:60-70`) sets `Visit`, moves to `VisitState.Recorded`, and snackbars only on a first visit (`Visit?.AlreadyVisited == false`, `:64-66`).
  - The refusal map (`:72-74`) is a ternary, not a switch: only `CheckInErrorCodes.EventNotPublished` is distinguished; everything else is `Unavailable`. There is no room-style "nothing scheduled here" case, because a booth is not on a schedule.
  - `FormatVisitedOn` (`:92-93`) is the local-time formatter, identical in mechanism to the room page's, with the same reason recorded in its XML comment (`:86-89`).
  - `Dispose(bool)` / `Dispose()` (`:113-133`) are the same disposable pattern over `_cts`.
- **Why it's built this way**: the class comment (`SponsorVisit.razor.cs:9-19`) is explicit that the server is idempotent per sponsor regardless of the guard, so the interactive-only latch is about not making a pointless round trip rather than about correctness. Recording the visit without a button press is the point of the surface: a sponsor booth interaction that costs an attendee two taps is an interaction most attendees will not complete, which is the engagement argument [ADR-072](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/072-qr-badge-check-in-and-points.html) records for attendee-self-recorded scan surfaces.
- **Where it's used**: the target of the sponsor QR rendered on the Conference sponsor detail page (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.UI/Pages/Sponsor/SponsorDetail.razor:93`, a `QrCodeButton` over [`ConferenceRoutePaths`](group-21-conference-ui.md#conferenceroutepaths)`.SponsorVisitLink`, `ConferenceRoutePaths.cs:55`). The post lands on [`CheckInsController`](#checkinscontroller)'s sponsor-visits endpoint (`CheckInsController.cs:114`, feature-gated on `EngagementFeatures.SponsorVisits`, see [`EngagementFeatures`](#engagementfeatures) and [ADR-031](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/031-feature-flag-management.html)) and is handled by [`RecordSponsorVisitHandler`](#recordsponsorvisithandler). Component-tested by [`SponsorVisitTests`](group-27-testing-infrastructure.md#sponsorvisittests).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the confirmation card, the points hint and the "already visited" copy live in `SponsorVisit.razor` and its resources. How many points a visit earns is decided server-side by the points earn rules, not here.

### EventFeedback

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Pages.Feedback` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Pages/Feedback/EventFeedback.razor.cs:16` · Level 8 · class (partial)

- **What it is**: the code-behind for the attendee event-feedback page (`@page "/feedback/event/{EventId}"`, `EventFeedback.razor:1`). It loads the event name and its dynamic question set, pre-fills any answers the attendee already submitted, and persists each answer as a POST-as-upsert (BR-107, per the class doc comment at `EventFeedback.razor.cs:12-15`).
- **Depends on**: [`IQuestionLookupService`](#iquestionlookupservice), [`IEventFeedbackUIService`](#ieventfeedbackuiservice), [`IEventLookupService`](group-21-conference-ui.md#ieventlookupservice), [`QuestionDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#questiondto), [`EventQuestionAnswerDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#eventquestionanswerdto), its nested [`AnswerState`](#answerstate), and [`DomainHelper`](group-02-domain-building-blocks.md#domainhelper) (the `Parse<T>` extension). Externals: Blazor `NavigationManager`, MudBlazor `ISnackbar` / `MudForm` / `BreadcrumbItem`, `System.Globalization`, and from the markup an injected `IStringLocalizer<EventFeedback>` `L` (`EventFeedback.razor:6`) plus the shared `UnsavedChangesGuard` component (`EventFeedback.razor:10-13`).
- **Concept introduced, the code-behind page over a dynamic question set.** `[Rubric §18, UI Architecture]` assesses component/logic separation: the markup lives in `EventFeedback.razor` and the behavior in this `partial class`, so the page's flow is reviewable apart from its layout. `[Rubric §24, Forms, Validation & UX Safety]` assesses form correctness and loss prevention: the page validates through `MudForm` before submitting, and wraps the whole form in `UnsavedChangesGuard IsDirty="_isDirty"` so navigating away with unsaved edits prompts first. The dynamic part is the interesting one: the questions are data, not markup, so the render is a `switch` over `question.QuestionType` (`EventFeedback.razor:44-68`) that picks a `MudRating`, an email `MudTextField`, or a 4000-character multiline `MudTextField`, and the per-question `Required`/`RequiredError` come from the DTO rather than from the page. `[Rubric §19, State Management]` covers the `_answers` working map; `[Rubric §27, i18n]` covers the fact that every visible string is an `L["..."]` lookup.
- **Walkthrough** (teaching order)
  - Injected services (`EventFeedback.razor.cs:18-22`) and the `[Parameter] string EventId` (`:24`) arrive from the route. Note the parameter is a `string`, not an id alias: the route template carries no `:int` constraint, so parsing is the page's job.
  - `IsLoading` / `IsSubmitting` (`:30-31`), `HasExistingFeedback` (`:33`) and `SubmitButtonText` (`:35-46`) drive the chrome: the button reads "Submitting" while a post is in flight, then "Update" or "Submit" depending on whether prior answers exist. The private state fields (`:48-55`) hold the load error, the event name, the loaded questions, the `_answers` map, the `_existingAnswerIds` map, the `MudForm` reference and the `_isDirty` flag. `MarkDirty` (`:56`) is the one-line handler every input's `@bind-...:after` calls.
  - `OnInitializedAsync` (`:58`) builds the breadcrumb trail, resolves the event name via `EventLookup.GetAllAsync` and sets a localized `_loadError` when the id is not in the lookup (`:70-80`), loads the `"Event"` questions ordered by `Sort` (`:83-84`), seeds one [`AnswerState`](#answerstate) per question (`:87`), then pre-fills from the server (`:90-91`). Every await passes `_cts.Token`; `OperationCanceledException` is swallowed as expected on disposal (`:93-96`), any other exception becomes a localized `_loadError` (`:97-100`), and `IsLoading` is cleared in `finally` (`:101-104`).
  - `PreFillExistingAnswers` (`:107`) is defense in depth. Because feedback questions are shared across events, an answer belonging to a different event would still match by `QuestionId`, so the loop skips any answer whose `EventId` does not equal the current one (`:113`) before mapping a rating (culture-invariant `int.TryParse`, `:125-129`) or a text value onto the holder. It also records the server-assigned answer id in `_existingAnswerIds` (`:123`), which is what enables the per-question clear button.
  - `SubmitFeedbackAsync` (`:137`) validates the form and snackbars a warning if it is invalid (`:139-147`), then projects the answered questions into a `pending` list, dropping blanks (`:157-160`). The loop (`:163-175`) is deliberately failure aware: it posts one upsert per answered question, counts successes, and on the first failure reports exactly how much was saved (`Snackbar.PartialSubmit` with `submitted` and `pending.Count`, `:172`) and returns while leaving `_isDirty` set, so the same Submit button retries and re-upserting an already-saved answer is a no-op. On a clean pass it clears `_isDirty`, snackbars the count, then calls `StateHasChanged()` **before** `NavigateBack()` (`:180-181`); the inline comment (`:179`) explains why: the flush lets `UnsavedChangesGuard`'s `IsDirty` parameter update before `NavigateTo` fires the `LocationChanging` handler, otherwise the guard would prompt over changes that were just saved.
  - `DeleteAnswerAsync` (`:197`) clears one previously-saved answer: it looks the answer id up in `_existingAnswerIds`, calls `FeedbackService.DeleteAnswerAsync`, removes the entry, resets the holder's two members (`:208-212`), and clears `_isDirty` because the delete already committed server-side (`:214-215`).
  - `ParseEventId` (`:228-229`) converts the route string with `EventId.Parse<EventIdentifierType>()`, the [`DomainHelper`](group-02-domain-building-blocks.md#domainhelper) extension, rather than a hand-rolled parse. `NavigateBack` (`:231-232`) returns to the Conference event detail route.
  - `Dispose(bool)` / `Dispose()` (`:236-256`) implement the standard disposable pattern, cancelling and disposing `_cts` so in-flight loads stop when the attendee leaves.
- **Why it's built this way**: the page owns orchestration only, all I/O goes through injected UI service interfaces, which keeps it testable under bUnit and swappable per host. Two details are correctness, not style: the cancellation-token discipline on every await, and the `StateHasChanged`-before-navigate ordering, which is the documented fix for the guard's stale `IsDirty` parameter. The per-question upsert loop, rather than one batch call, is what makes a partial failure recoverable by pressing the same button again.
- **Where it's used**: rendered at `/feedback/event/{EventId}`, reached from Conference event pages via [`EngagementRoutePaths`](#engagementroutepaths)`.EventFeedback(eventId)`. Its dependencies are registered by the Engagement UI [`DependencyInjection`](#dependencyinjection). Component-tested by [`EventFeedbackTests`](group-27-testing-infrastructure.md#eventfeedbacktests).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the markup, the resource strings and `UnsavedChangesGuard`'s own behavior live outside this file; this section covers the code-behind plus the bindings its members exist to serve.

### SessionFeedback

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Pages.Feedback` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Pages/Feedback/SessionFeedback.razor.cs:16` · Level 8 · class (partial)

- **What it is**: the code-behind for the attendee session-feedback page (`@page "/feedback/session/{SessionId}"`, `SessionFeedback.razor:1`). It mirrors [`EventFeedback`](#eventfeedback) but first enforces three business preconditions before it will render the question form at all.
- **Depends on**: [`IQuestionLookupService`](#iquestionlookupservice), [`ISessionFeedbackUIService`](#isessionfeedbackuiservice), [`IEntityService<TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#ientityservicetentitydto-tidentifiertype) (bound as `IEntityService<SessionDTO, SessionIdentifierType>`, `SessionFeedback.razor.cs:20`), [`SessionDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#sessiondto), [`QuestionDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#questiondto), [`SessionQuestionAnswerDTO`](group-17-conference-domain.md#sessionquestionanswerdto), its nested [`AnswerState`](#answerstate), and [`DomainHelper`](group-02-domain-building-blocks.md#domainhelper). Externals as on [`EventFeedback`](#eventfeedback) (`NavigationManager`, `ISnackbar`, `MudForm`, `IStringLocalizer<SessionFeedback>`, `UnsavedChangesGuard` at `SessionFeedback.razor:10`).
- **Concept, precondition-gated feedback.** The submission and pre-fill machinery is identical to [`EventFeedback`](#eventfeedback) (the same `_answers` map, `SubmitFeedbackAsync`, `DeleteAnswerAsync`, `GetAnswerValue`, `Dispose`, and the same `StateHasChanged`-before-`NavigateBack` ordering at `SessionFeedback.razor.cs:211-213`), so that shape is not re-taught here. What distinguishes this page is `CheckPreconditions`, which is a `[Rubric §24, Forms, Validation & UX Safety]` story before it is anything else: when feedback is not permitted the attendee gets one sentence explaining why, not a form that fails on submit. `[Rubric §11, Security]` applies in the weaker sense that the page never renders inputs for a write the server would refuse; the rules are enforced server-side too, this is the client half.
- **Walkthrough of what differs from [`EventFeedback`](#eventfeedback)**
  - One extra field, `_preconditionMessage` (`SessionFeedback.razor.cs:49`), sits beside `_loadError` so the markup can distinguish "we could not load this" from "feedback is not open for this session".
  - `OnInitializedAsync` (`:59`) loads the session through `SessionService.GetByIdAsync(sessionId, false, _cts.Token)` (`:73`), a generic [`IEntityService<TEntityDTO, TIdentifierType>`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#ientityservicetentitydto-tidentifiertype) rather than a purpose-built lookup service, and sets `_loadError` when the session is null (`:75-78`). It then runs `CheckPreconditions(session)` and, if a message comes back, stores it and returns **before** loading any questions (`:82-87`).
  - `CheckPreconditions` (`:114`) encodes three rules, each returning a localized message: BR-91 rejects service sessions (`session.IsServiceSession`, `:117-119`); BR-49 rejects sessions whose `Status` is set and is not `"Accepted"`, compared case-insensitively (`:123-126`); BR-16 rejects unscheduled sessions, where `StartsAt` or `EndsAt` is null (`:131-134`), because session feedback is a time-gated action. Returning `null` means feedback is allowed.
  - `PreFillExistingAnswers` (`:139`) applies the same cross-entity guard as the event page, skipping answers whose `SessionId` does not match the current session (`:145`).
  - `ParseSessionId` (`:261-262`) uses `SessionId.Parse<SessionIdentifierType>()`; `NavigateBack` (`:264-265`) returns to the Conference session detail route.
  - The duplicated helpers are byte-for-byte the event page's: `GetAnswerValue` (`:291-301`) and the nested [`AnswerState`](#answerstate) (`:304`).
- **Why it's built this way**: pushing the three business rules into one `CheckPreconditions` method keeps the render path binary (either a precondition message or the form) and makes each BR individually reviewable and individually testable. Sharing the submission shape with [`EventFeedback`](#eventfeedback) by convention rather than through a shared base class keeps each page self-contained and independently editable, the same trade-off made for the duplicated [`AnswerState`](#answerstate).
- **Where it's used**: rendered at `/feedback/session/{SessionId}`, linked from Conference session detail pages via [`EngagementRoutePaths`](#engagementroutepaths)`.SessionFeedback(sessionId)`. Component-tested by [`SessionFeedbackTests`](group-27-testing-infrastructure.md#sessionfeedbacktests).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: as with [`EventFeedback`](#eventfeedback), the markup and resource strings live in `SessionFeedback.razor`. The BR numbers in the comments are the page's own claim about which business rule it implements; the authoritative statements live in the ADC specifications guide, not in this file.

### AttendeeRow

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/AttendeeLookupService.cs:170` · Level 0 · record (private sealed, nested)

- **What it is**: the wire shape of one row returned by the Identity `/Users` endpoint, declared privately inside [`AttendeeLookupService`](#attendeelookupservice) purely so `System.Text.Json` has something to bind to. It never leaves the service: every row is projected into the public [`AttendeeSummary`](#attendeesummary) before it reaches a caller.
- **Depends on**: the `UserIdentifierType` alias (solution-wide `global using`, see [primer §2](00-primer.md#2-architectural-styles-this-codebase-commits-to)). Externals: `System.Net.Http.Json` binding at the call site, wrapped in [`PagedCollectionResult<T>`](group-01-result-error-handling.md#pagedcollectionresultt).
- **Concept introduced, the private deserialization shape.** `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses how a consumer expresses the contract it reads. Two types for one payload looks redundant until you read what the private one is allowed to be: the doc comment (`AttendeeLookupService.cs:166-169`) records that only the fields the check-in surfaces render are read and that the device and audit fields the endpoint may carry are ignored, which is exactly the tolerant-reader posture. `[Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness]` is the other half: Engagement redeclares five fields instead of referencing the Identity assembly for its list DTO ([`IAttendeeLookupService`](#iattendeelookupservice)`.cs:4-7` states that constraint for the public twin), so Identity can move, be versioned, or add columns without a compile-time edit here.
- **Walkthrough** (`AttendeeLookupService.cs:170-175`): five positional members, `UserId`, `Email`, `FirstName`, `LastName`, `Role`, structurally identical to [`AttendeeSummary`](#attendeesummary) minus its computed `DisplayName`. It is bound as `PagedCollectionResult<AttendeeRow>` (`AttendeeLookupService.cs:86`) so one read picks up both the page items and the pagination metadata, and converted one row at a time by the private `ToSummary` (`:163-164`).
- **Why it's built this way**: `private sealed` keeps the wire shape from becoming an accidental public contract. The projection point is the single place where a server rename would have to be absorbed, and the computed display fallback lives on the public record rather than on the row, so the transport type stays inert data.
- **Where it's used**: only inside [`AttendeeLookupService`](#attendeelookupservice), by `SearchAsync` (`:86-88`) and, through it, by the roster snapshot `LoadRosterAsync` builds (`:142-152`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: nothing at compile time keeps this shape aligned with what Identity actually serializes. A renamed server property binds to the member default rather than failing the build.

### AttendeeSearchField

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/IAttendeeLookupService.cs:39` · Level 0 · enum

- **What it is**: which one of last name, first name or email the organizer's single search box applies to when looking an attendee up at the door.
- **Depends on**: nothing (a bare `enum`).
- **Concept introduced, the enum that exists because of a server semantic.** `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses whether a client contract makes the server's actual behavior usable. The type doc states the reason outright (`IAttendeeLookupService.cs:34-38`): the Identity users endpoint **ANDs** its filters, so broadcasting one typed term across all three fields would match nobody. A UI that offered one box and no field picker would therefore be a UI that silently never finds anyone. `[Rubric §24, Forms, Validation & UX Safety]` covers the resulting shape: one box plus one explicit selector, so the organizer always knows which field the term is being matched against.
- **Walkthrough** (`IAttendeeLookupService.cs:39-49`): three members with explicit values, `LastName = 0` (`:42`), `FirstName = 1` (`:45`), `Email = 2` (`:48`). The explicit ordinals matter because the value is round-tripped as a string through the grid's filter state and parsed back with `Enum.TryParse` ([`AttendeeSearchPanel`](#attendeesearchpanel)`.razor.cs:49-51`), which falls back to `LastName` when the stored value does not parse. `LastName` is also the field's initial value (`AttendeeSearchPanel.razor.cs:36`), which is the field an organizer reads off a badge first.
- **Why it's built this way**: an enum rather than a raw string keeps the three-way mapping exhaustive and reviewable in one `switch` (`AttendeeSearchPanel.razor.cs:112-118`), which projects the selected member onto the matching [`AttendeeSummary`](#attendeesummary) property name and writes a single `("contains", term)` filter (`:120`).
- **Where it's used**: only by [`AttendeeSearchPanel`](#attendeesearchpanel), as the bound value of a `MudSelect` (`AttendeeSearchPanel.razor:16-22`) and as the discriminator in `ApplySearchTerm`; the resulting filter is what [`IAttendeeLookupService`](#iattendeelookupservice)`.SearchAsync` receives as its `email` / `firstName` / `lastName` arguments.

### CheckInErrorCodes

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/CheckInErrorCodes.cs:8` · Level 0 · static class

- **What it is**: the two stable error codes the self-service QR landing pages branch on, held as constants beside the service that reads them instead of being retyped inside each page.
- **Depends on**: nothing (two `const string` fields). Consumed together with [`SelfCheckInOutcome<TResult>`](#selfcheckinoutcometresult), which carries the code back from the transport layer.
- **Concept introduced, the error code as a client-facing contract.** `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses whether failures are expressed in a form a client can branch on. HTTP status codes are not enough here and the type doc says why by implication: two of the three refusals arrive as the same 404, so a page choosing its state from the status alone cannot tell "nothing is scheduled in this room" from "this feature is off". A stable machine-readable code in the ProblemDetails body is the only distinguishing signal, and it must never be a human-readable message, because that message is localized and would break the branch in any non-English locale. `[Rubric §10, Cross-Cutting]` covers the placement: one declaration site for the vocabulary means a page never spells a code out inline, so a typo is a compile error rather than a silently unreachable branch.
- **Walkthrough** (`CheckInErrorCodes.cs:8-18`): `EventNotPublished = "CheckIns.EventNotPublished"` (`:11`) means the owning event is not published yet, so nothing is being recorded for it. `NoCurrentSession = "CheckIns.NoCurrentSession"` (`:17`) means nothing is scheduled in the scanned room at this instant, and the member comment (`:13-16`) records the security decision folded into it: an unknown room answers the same way by design, so the state never confirms which room identifiers exist.
- **Why it's built this way**: the deliberate absence is as informative as the presence. There is no constant for "feature disabled" or "unknown target", because those produce a bare 404 with no code, which both pages treat as their generic unavailable state ([`RoomCheckIn`](#roomcheckin)`.razor.cs:69-74`, [`SponsorVisit`](#sponsorvisit)`.razor.cs:72-74`). Refusing to name them is what keeps a printed code from confirming which sponsors or rooms exist ([ADR-072](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/072-qr-badge-check-in-and-points.html)).
- **Where it's used**: read by [`RoomCheckIn`](#roomcheckin) and [`SponsorVisit`](#sponsorvisit) when mapping an outcome onto their page state; produced on the server as literal strings by [`RecordSponsorVisitHandler`](#recordsponsorvisithandler) (`RecordSponsorVisitHandler.cs:67`), [`RecordRoomCheckInHandler`](#recordroomcheckinhandler) (`RecordRoomCheckInHandler.cs:70`, `:125`) and [`CheckInProcessor`](#checkinprocessor) (`CheckInProcessor.cs:128`), and documented on the endpoint itself ([`CheckInsController`](#checkinscontroller)`.cs:134`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the two sides of this contract are separate string literals in separate processes, not one shared constant, because the UI project deliberately does not reference the Application layer. Nothing but a test keeps them equal.

### NowNextSessionInfo

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/INowNextService.cs:25` · Level 0 · sealed record

- **What it is**: one row of the now-and-next snapshot, a session's identifier, title, room name, and its wall-clock start and end in the event's own time zone.
- **Depends on**: the `SessionIdentifierType` alias (solution-wide `global using`, see [primer §2](00-primer.md#2-architectural-styles-this-codebase-commits-to)). Externals: BCL `DateTime`, plus `System.Text.Json` name-based binding at the call site.
- **Concept, the locally mirrored wire shape.** `[Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness]` assesses whether a module can be lifted out without dragging a neighbor's assembly along, and `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` how a payload contract is expressed to its consumers. The bytes on the wire are produced by Conference's [NowNextSessionDTO](group-17-conference-domain.md#nownextsessiondto); Engagement redeclares only the fields it renders instead of referencing `MMCA.ADC.Conference.Shared` for one payload, and JSON binds them by property name. The type doc records that this is a deliberate mirror and that the Android home-screen widget makes the same call with a mirror of its own (`INowNextService.cs:4-7`; the widget's private copy is at `MMCA.ADC/Source/Hosts/UI/MMCA.ADC.UI/Platforms/Android/NowNextWidgetProvider.cs:130-134`). The same tolerant-reader trade-off taught on [AttendeeRow](#attendeerow) applies: nothing at compile time keeps the mirror aligned with the DTO, only the round-trip tests do.
- **Walkthrough** (`INowNextService.cs:25-30`): five positional members, `SessionId` (line 26, the deep-link target), `Title` (27), `RoomName` (28, nullable), `StartsAtLocal` (29), and `EndsAtLocal` (30). Two details are load-bearing. `RoomName` is null whenever the session carries no room id, or carries one the event's room map does not contain, a decision the server makes in a single expression (`GetNowNextHandler.cs:84`). And the two times are plain `DateTime` wall clock in the event's zone, not UTC and not `DateTimeOffset`: the server passes the stored wall-clock values straight through (`GetNowNextHandler.cs:85-86`) and the page formats them with the short-time pattern under the current culture (`HappeningNow.razor.cs:369-370`), so no client ever converts a zone.
- **Why it's built this way**: glanceable surfaces (a page card, a home-screen widget) should print exactly what the printed schedule says, so the wall-clock value travels as data. The UTC instants stay on the server's side of the contract for callers that do their own math (`GetNowNextHandler.cs:87-88`); this mirror deliberately drops them, along with `EventId`. The slice arrives with [ADR-042](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/042-device-capability-abstraction.html) Wave 8 (`GetNowNextHandler.cs:13`).
- **Where it's used**: only inside [NowNextSnapshot](#nownextsnapshot)'s `Now` and `Next` lists; the [HappeningNow](group-23-engagement-live-layer.md#happeningnow) page holds them in `_sessionsNow` / `_sessionsNext` (`HappeningNow.razor.cs:48-49`) and renders one card per row.
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the mirror is checked by [NowNextServiceTests](group-27-testing-infrastructure.md#nownextservicetests) round-tripping a JSON body, not by the compiler, so a renamed server property would deserialize to a default value rather than break the build.

### SelfCheckInOutcome<TResult>

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/SelfCheckInOutcome.cs:20` · Level 0 · sealed record (generic)

- **What it is**: the return shape of one self-service check-in post, a sponsor booth visit or a room check-in. It carries either the recorded payload or the server's stable error code, so the calling page can pick its state without catching an exception.
- **Depends on**: nothing first-party; it is closed over [`SponsorVisitResultDTO`](#sponsorvisitresultdto) and [`RoomCheckInResultDTO`](#roomcheckinresultdto) at the two call sites, and its `ErrorCode` values come from [`CheckInErrorCodes`](#checkinerrorcodes). Externals: BCL only, with a `where TResult : class` constraint (`SelfCheckInOutcome.cs:21`) so the nullable `Value` means exactly "absent".
- **Concept introduced, the result envelope for a refusal that is not a fault.** `[Rubric §1, SOLID]` and `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` both apply: the type doc (`SelfCheckInOutcome.cs:3-13`) explains that the landing pages must tell three server answers apart (no session running in this room, event not published, and the flat "unavailable" a disabled feature or unknown target produces), that all three arrive as ordinary non-success responses, and that **two of them share the same 404 status**. A `Result<T>` carrying only a message would force the page to parse localized prose; an exception would force a `try`/`catch` around a normal outcome. Carrying the code as data is what makes the page's mapping a `switch` expression over constants. Note this is deliberately *not* the framework's [Result<T>](group-01-result-error-handling.md#resultt): this is a UI-layer transport envelope for one narrow HTTP pattern, so it stays small and local rather than reusing a domain abstraction it does not need.
- **Walkthrough** (`SelfCheckInOutcome.cs:20-25`): two positional members, `Value` (the recorded outcome, null when the server refused) and `ErrorCode` (null when the response carried none: a bare feature-gate 404 or an unparseable body, per the param doc at `:16-19`). One computed member, `IsSuccess => Value is not null` (`:24`), so success is derived from the payload rather than tracked as a third field that could disagree with it. Construction is centralized in [`CheckInService`](#checkinservice)`.PostSelfCheckInAsync` (`CheckInService.cs:121-140`): the success branch deserializes `TResult` and pairs it with a null code (`:135-136`), the failure branch passes null with whatever `ReadErrorCodeAsync` extracted from the ProblemDetails body (`:139`, `:147`).
- **Why it's built this way**: the two organizer writes on the same service throw through `ServiceExceptionHelper` (`CheckInService.cs:106-107`) because an organizer facing a broken scan wants a fault surfaced. The two attendee-facing self-service writes must not, because every refusal is a sentence the landing page renders calmly to someone holding a phone in a hallway. The method doc records exactly that split (`CheckInService.cs:113-120`). Keeping the generic on the payload lets one private helper serve both endpoints with no duplication ([ADR-072](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/072-qr-badge-check-in-and-points.html)).
- **Where it's used**: returned by [`ICheckInUIService`](#icheckinuiservice)`.RecordSponsorVisitAsync` and `.RecordRoomCheckInAsync` (`ICheckInUIService.cs:43`, `:53`), consumed by [`SponsorVisit`](#sponsorvisit) and [`RoomCheckIn`](#roomcheckin).

### SessionReminder

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/SessionReminderPlanner.cs:13` · Level 0 · sealed record

- **What it is**: an immutable value describing one planned on-device reminder for a bookmarked session, a stable notification id, when it should fire, and where a tap takes the user.
- **Depends on**: the `SessionIdentifierType` alias (solution-wide `global using`, see [primer §2](00-primer.md#2-architectural-styles-this-codebase-commits-to)). Externals: BCL `DateTimeOffset`. No first-party types beyond the alias.
- **Concept, the immutable planning DTO.** `[Rubric §19, State Management]` assesses whether client-side state is modeled as explicit, predictable values rather than mutable ad-hoc fields. A `sealed record` gives value equality and `init`-only positional members for free (see [records in the primer](00-primer.md#c-records--read-this-once)), so a planned reminder is a snapshot that cannot drift after [`SessionReminderPlanner`](#sessionreminderplanner) computes it. The type carries no behavior: it is pure data handed from the pure planner to the stateful coordinator.
- **Walkthrough** (`SessionReminderPlanner.cs:13-19`): six positional members, `NotificationId` (int, line 14), `SessionId` (15), `SessionTitle` (16), `StartsAtUtc` (17), `DeliverAt` (18), and `DeepLinkRoute` (19). The doc comment (`:3-12`) is load-bearing intent: `NotificationId` is derived deterministically from the session id so rescheduling **replaces** an existing OS notification instead of stacking a duplicate, `DeliverAt` is the fire instant (start minus lead, clamped to now), and `DeepLinkRoute` is the app-relative route published on tap.
- **Why it's built this way**: keeping the planned reminder as a value lets the planner stay a pure function and the coordinator diff plans by id ([ADR-042](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/042-device-capability-abstraction.html) Wave 2, the local-notification dispatcher).
- **Where it's used**: produced by [`SessionReminderPlanner`](#sessionreminderplanner)`.Plan` (`SessionReminderPlanner.cs:79-85`); consumed by [`SessionReminderCoordinator`](#sessionremindercoordinator), which turns each one into a [`LocalNotificationRequest`](group-26-device-capability-layer.md#localnotificationrequest) (`SessionReminderCoordinator.cs:171-177`).

### NowNextSnapshot

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/INowNextService.cs:13` · Level 1 · sealed record

- **What it is**: the whole "happening now / up next" payload for one event as the Engagement UI consumes it, the event's display name, whether the event is live at the query instant, and two lists of [NowNextSessionInfo](#nownextsessioninfo) rows.
- **Depends on**: [NowNextSessionInfo](#nownextsessioninfo). Externals: BCL `IReadOnlyList<T>`.
- **Concept, the two-bucket time projection, computed once on the server.** `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]` assesses whether work sits where it is cheapest, and `[Rubric §18, UI Architecture]` whether a page renders a prepared view model rather than deriving one. Both buckets are decided by [GetNowNextHandler](group-18-conference-application.md#getnownexthandler) against a single instant taken from an injected `TimeProvider` (`GetNowNextHandler.cs:22`, `:29`), never from the browser clock. `Now` is every eligible session whose window is half-open around that instant, `StartsAtUtc <= utcNow && utcNow < EndsAtUtc` (`GetNowNextHandler.cs:59`), so a session that ends exactly now is already out and the one starting exactly now is already in. `Next` is not "the next session": it is the batch that shares the earliest future start (`:64-72`), which is what makes parallel tracks appear together instead of one arbitrary room winning. The empty cases are ordinary, not error states: between sessions `Now` is empty, after the last session `Next` is empty (`nextStart` is null and the list is `[]`, `:66-68`), and before the first session `Now` is empty while `Next` carries the opening batch.
- **Walkthrough** (`INowNextService.cs:13-17`): four positional members, `EventName` (line 14), `IsLive` (15), `Now` (16), and `Next` (17). `IsLive` is independent of the two lists: the server compares the same instant against the event's live window from [CurrentEventSelector](group-17-conference-domain.md#currenteventselector)`.GetLiveWindowUtc(StartDate, EndDate, TimeZone)` (`GetNowNextHandler.cs:74-75`), so a live event during a coffee break reports `IsLive: true` with an empty `Now`. Ordering is fixed server-side too: `Now` sorts by start then by room name ordinal-ignore-case (`:60-61`), `Next` by room name alone (`:71`) since every row in it shares one start.
- **Why it's built this way**: no client can compute either bucket correctly on its own, because eligibility depends on data a public catalog read does not expose (see [INowNextService](#inownextservice)), so the snapshot is shipped precomputed and every surface (page and widget) shows the same answer. The one snapshot type also keeps `IsLive` next to the lists, which is what lets a caller distinguish "the conference is not running" from "nothing is on right now".
- **Where it's used**: returned (nullable) by [INowNextService](#inownextservice)`.GetAsync`; the [HappeningNow](group-23-engagement-live-layer.md#happeningnow) page projects it with `snapshot?.Now ?? []` and `snapshot?.Next ?? []` (`HappeningNow.razor.cs:185-186`), so a null snapshot renders the same empty state as an empty list. Conference's own wire type is [NowNextDTO](group-17-conference-domain.md#nownextdto) (`GetNowNextHandler.cs:77`), and the Android widget deserializes a third copy of the shape ([NowNextWidgetProvider](group-25-adc-host-composition.md#nownextwidgetprovider)`.cs:127`, `:132`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: nothing on the record marks how fresh it is. Staleness is bounded by two caches only, the query result cache of 30 seconds ([GetNowNextQuery](group-18-conference-application.md#getnownextquery) implements [IQueryCacheable](group-05-cqrs-pipeline.md#iquerycacheable), `GetNowNextQuery.cs:38`) and the endpoint's 60-second `NowNextCache` output-cache policy (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Services/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Service/Program.cs:246`). The page fetches the snapshot once from `LoadSessionsAsync` (`HappeningNow.razor.cs:183`) and has no timer of its own, so a long-open tab keeps its first now-and-next lists until something refreshes it.

### SessionReminderPlanner

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/SessionReminderPlanner.cs:29` · Level 1 · static class

- **What it is**: a pure, side-effect-free planner that turns a set of bookmarked sessions plus an event time zone and a lead time into a list of [`SessionReminder`](#sessionreminder) values. All the date math (wall-clock to UTC, DST edge cases, lead-window clamping) lives here.
- **Depends on**: [`SessionReminder`](#sessionreminder), [`SessionInfo`](group-23-engagement-live-layer.md#sessioninfo) (the input session shape), [`EngagementRoutePaths`](#engagementroutepaths) (the tap route), and the `SessionIdentifierType` alias. Externals: BCL `TimeZoneInfo`, `DateTimeOffset`.
- **Concept, the deterministic pure function.** `[Rubric §14, Testability]` assesses whether logic can be exercised without infrastructure. Every input the planner needs is a parameter, including `now` (`SessionReminderPlanner.cs:51`), the current instant, which the caller supplies rather than the planner reading `DateTimeOffset.UtcNow` itself. That single choice makes DST behavior, lead-window clamping and skip rules unit-testable with fixed clocks and no OS notification stack. `[Rubric §27, Internationalization]` also applies: session times are wall-clock local to the event's IANA time zone, never UTC in the DTOs (`SessionInfo.StartsAt` is a bare `DateTime?`, `ISessionLookupService.cs:10`), so correct conversion is a first-class concern here rather than an afterthought.
- **Walkthrough**:
  - `NotificationIdSeed` (`:34`) is a private constant offset that keeps reminder ids clear of any other local-notification ids the app might use. The comment above it (`:31-33`) states the assumption the whole replace-by-id contract rests on: `SessionIdentifierType` hash codes are stable for value types.
  - `GetNotificationId(sessionId)` (`:37-38`) shifts the seed 16 bits and XORs the session id's hash `unchecked`, producing that stable id.
  - `Plan(bookmarkedSessions, timeZoneId, leadTime, now)` (`:47-89`) guards its arguments (`:53-54`), resolves the `TimeZoneInfo` (`:56`), then per session skips those with no start time (`:61-64`) or already started (`:67-70`), computes `deliverAt = startsAtUtc - leadTime` and, when that instant has already passed, fires an immediate reminder 30 seconds out rather than dropping it (`:72-77`), and finally emits a [`SessionReminder`](#sessionreminder) whose tap route comes from [`EngagementRoutePaths`](#engagementroutepaths)`.SessionDetails` (`:79-85`, `EngagementRoutePaths.cs:12`).
  - `ToUtc(localWallClock, timeZone)` (`:91-104`) is the DST-correct converter: it marks the wall time `Unspecified` (`:93`), shifts a spring-forward **invalid** time forward one hour (`:96-99`), and lets `GetUtcOffset` resolve ambiguous fall-back times to the standard offset (`:101-103`).
- **Why it's built this way**: separating planning (pure, here) from scheduling (I/O, in the coordinator) means the tricky calendar logic is provable in isolation and the coordinator stays a thin best-effort orchestrator ([ADR-042](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/042-device-capability-abstraction.html) Wave 2).
- **Where it's used**: called by [`SessionReminderCoordinator`](#sessionremindercoordinator)`.ReplanAsync` (`SessionReminderCoordinator.cs:147-151`); `GetNotificationId` is also called there to cancel reminders for sessions that left the tracked set (`:98`) or dropped out of the plan (`:160-163`). Covered by [SessionReminderPlannerTests](group-27-testing-infrastructure.md#sessionreminderplannertests).

### CurrentEventNotificationScopeProvider

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/CurrentEventNotificationScopeProvider.cs:23` · Level 2 · sealed partial class

- **What it is**: ADC's implementation of the framework's notification scope hook. It answers "which conference event is in focus right now" as the opaque string `event:{EventId}`, so a notification sent for an event that has since ended stops surfacing once the next event becomes the current one.
- **Depends on**: [`INotificationScopeProvider`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#inotificationscopeprovider) (the framework contract it implements), [`ILiveEventUIService`](group-23-engagement-live-layer.md#iliveeventuiservice) and [`LiveEventContext`](group-23-engagement-live-layer.md#liveeventcontext) (the resolution source). Externals: `TimeProvider`, `System.Threading.Lock`, `ILogger<T>` with a source-generated `[LoggerMessage]`, and `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture`.
- **Concept introduced, the app-supplied scope over a framework-owned mechanism.** `[Rubric §3, Clean Architecture]` and `[Rubric §10, Cross-Cutting]` assess whether a cross-cutting concern is expressed as a framework extension point the application fills in rather than as application logic baked into the framework. MMCA.Common knows notifications carry an optional `ScopeKey` ([ADR-024](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/024-push-notifications.html)) and nothing more; the meaning of that string is ADC's business, and the type doc says so directly (`CurrentEventNotificationScopeProvider.cs:10-11`: to the framework it is just an opaque string). `[Rubric §11, Security]` is the constraint that shapes the failure path: the inline comment at `:59` states that unscoped is the safe direction because a scope is a **view filter, never a security boundary**. Failing open therefore restores the pre-scope behavior instead of hiding an attendee's inbox, which is the opposite of what a security check should do on failure and exactly right for a filter.
- **Walkthrough**:
  - The primary constructor (`:23-26`) takes the live-event service, a `TimeProvider` and a logger, and the class declares `INotificationScopeProvider`. `CacheDuration` is five minutes (`:28`).
  - The cache is two fields plus a `Lock` (`:33-35`). The comment above them (`:30-32`) is the concurrency reasoning worth reading: the lock guards the two fields **only**, the refresh runs outside it (a `Lock` cannot be held across an `await`), so two callers racing an expired cache both fetch. That is accepted deliberately, because the events read is idempotent and both callers converge on the same key, which beats serializing every poll behind a gate.
  - `GetCurrentScopeKeyAsync(ct)` (`:38`) takes the lock, returns the cached key while it is still fresh (`:40-46`), then resolves outside the lock: `liveEventService.GetCurrentEventAsync(ct)` (`:51`) and, when an event comes back, formats `event:{EventId}` with `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture` (`:54`) so the key never varies by locale.
  - The `catch` (`:57-61`) swallows everything, logs at Debug through `LogScopeResolutionFailed` (`:74-75`) and leaves `scopeKey` null.
  - The write-back (`:65-69`) caches **null like any other answer**, with the comment (`:63-64`) giving the cost model: a dormant or unreachable events API costs one fetch per five minutes rather than one per poll, and the next window retries.
- **Why it's built this way**: the caching is not premature. Both notification HTTP services ask for the scope on every call ([`NotificationInboxService`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#notificationinboxservice)`.cs:109`, [`PushNotificationService`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#pushnotificationservice)`.cs:34`) and [`NotificationBell`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#notificationbell) polls the unread count every 30 seconds (`NotificationBell.razor.cs:16`, `:46`), so an uncached provider would put an events fetch behind every poll. `[Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]` and `[Rubric §31, Cost/FinOps]` both land on that arithmetic. The registration is the other deliberate detail: `services.AddScoped<INotificationScopeProvider, CurrentEventNotificationScopeProvider>()` (`MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:66`) uses plain `AddScoped` so it overrides the [`NullNotificationScopeProvider`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#nullnotificationscopeprovider) that `AddNotificationUI` registers with `TryAddScoped`, which keeps the two calls order-independent (`DependencyInjection.cs:60-62`); the line above it (`:65`) has to `TryAddSingleton(TimeProvider.System)` because no UI head registers a `TimeProvider` (the heads never call `AddInfrastructure`).
- **Where it's used**: resolved by the framework's notification services listed above; never called directly by an ADC page. Covered by [CurrentEventNotificationScopeProviderTests](group-27-testing-infrastructure.md#currenteventnotificationscopeprovidertests).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: the cache is per instance and the registration is scoped, so on Blazor Server the window is per circuit, not per user or per process. How long a stale key can survive an event rollover is therefore bounded by the five-minute window, not by any invalidation signal.

### IBookmarkUIService

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/IBookmarkUIService.cs:9` · Level 2 · interface

- **What it is**: the UI-side contract for the `Bookmarks` API resource as the Engagement UI consumes it, create, list-by-user, and delete.
- **Depends on**: [`CreateBookmarkRequest`](#createbookmarkrequest), [`UserSessionBookmarkDTO`](#usersessionbookmarkdto), and the `UserIdentifierType` / `EventIdentifierType` / `UserSessionBookmarkIdentifierType` aliases. Externals: BCL `Task`.
- **Concept introduced, the UI service abstraction.** `[Rubric §18, UI Architecture]` assesses whether components talk to typed service interfaces rather than raw `HttpClient`s. Components depend on this interface, not on [`BookmarkService`](#bookmarkservice), so a page can be tested against a fake while the transport concerns (auth headers, retry policy, error translation) stay behind the contract in [`AuthenticatedServiceBase`](group-15-common-ui-framework.md#authenticatedservicebase). Every interface in this unit follows the same shape.
- **Walkthrough** (`IBookmarkUIService.cs:11-27`): `CreateAsync(request, ct)` returns a nullable [`UserSessionBookmarkDTO`](#usersessionbookmarkdto) (`:12-14`); `GetUserBookmarksAsync(userId, eventId?, pageNumber, pageSize, ct)` returns a tuple of the page items plus the total count (`:17-22`), with `eventId` optional and paging defaulted to `pageNumber = 1` / `pageSize = 10`; `DeleteAsync(id, ct)` returns a `bool` (`:25-27`) so a caller can tell a real delete from a not-found without catching an exception.
- **Why it's built this way**: the identifier-keyed shape (`UserSessionBookmarkIdentifierType` on delete, `userId` on read) mirrors the REST resource one-for-one, which keeps the service a thin translation of the endpoint rather than a second place where bookmark rules could accumulate.
- **Where it's used**: implemented by [`BookmarkService`](#bookmarkservice) (`BookmarkService.cs:17`) and registered scoped (`MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:26`).
- **Caveats / not-in-source**: no page or component in the repository injects this interface today. The session-level surfaces (the star toggle on the public session list and detail pages) go through the sibling [`ISessionBookmarkUIService`](#isessionbookmarkuiservice) instead, which is the one [`SessionBookmarkUIService`](#sessionbookmarkuiservice) implements and the Conference pages resolve optionally (`MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.UI/Pages/Public/PublicSessionList.razor.cs:114`). This contract stays registered and implemented, so it is available, but its consumers are not in source.

### ICheckInUIService

> MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · `MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services` · `MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/ICheckInUIService.cs:9` · Level 2 · interface

- **What it is**: the single UI contract for every QR badge check-in surface: the attendee's own badge, the organizer scan and manual fallback writes, the two attendee self-service scans, and the organizer attendance rollup.
- **Depends on**: [`MyBadgeDTO`](#mybadgedto), [`CheckInAttendeeRequest`](#checkinattendeerequest), [`ManualCheckInRequest`](#manualcheckinrequest), [`CheckInResultDTO`](#checkinresultdto), [`SelfCheckInOutcome<TResult>`](#selfcheckinoutcometresult), [`SponsorVisitResultDTO`](#sponsorvisitresultdto), [`RoomCheckInResultDTO`](#roomcheckinresultdto), [`AttendanceStatsDTO`](#attendancestatsdto), and the `SponsorIdentifierType` / `RoomIdentifierType` / `EventIdentifierType` aliases. The UI service abstraction itself is taught on [`IBookmarkUIService`](#ibookmarkuiservice).
- **Concept introduced, two error contracts in one interface.** `[Rubric §9, API & Contract Design]` assesses whether a contract's failure semantics are legible from its signatures, and this interface is the clearest example in the module because it deliberately carries **two**. Four methods return a nullable DTO, which is the ordinary shape here: null means the server said no such thing, and anything worse throws. Two methods return [`SelfCheckInOutcome<TResult>`](#selfcheckinoutcometresult), which never throws for a refusal, because those two are the endpoints an attendee reaches by scanning a printed code and every refusal has to render as a calm sentence. The class doc on [`CheckInService`](#checkinservice) (`CheckInService.cs:11-14`) states the other axis: every call is authenticated, the badge read is caller-scoped ("my badge"), and the three organizer calls are role-gated server-side, so `[Rubric §11, Security]` lives on the server, not in this contract.
- **Walkthrough** (`ICheckInUIService.cs:11-64`):
  - `GetMyBadgeAsync(ct)` (`:16`) fetches the signed-in attendee's own badge credential, null when the server returned none (`CheckInService.cs:22-38`, which maps a 404 to null at `:30-31`).
  - `CheckInByCredentialAsync(request, ct)` (`:23-25`) checks an attendee in from a scanned or typed badge payload; organizer only.
  - `ManualCheckInAsync(request, ct)` (`:33-35`) is the fallback used when a badge cannot be scanned; organizer only. Both post through the shared `PostCheckInAsync` (`CheckInService.cs:95`), whose doc (`:90-94`) records that a repeat check-in is reported through `AlreadyCheckedIn` rather than as a failure, so there is no conflict branch to interpret.
  - `RecordSponsorVisitAsync(sponsorId, ct)` (`:43-45`) records the caller's visit to one sponsor booth; the doc states a repeat visit is a success carrying `AlreadyVisited`, not a failure (`:38-40`).
  - `RecordRoomCheckInAsync(roomId, ct)` (`:53-55`) checks the caller into whatever session the scanned room is hosting right now. The doc sentence at `:48-50` is the whole design of that surface: **the session is never sent**, the server resolves it from the room plus the configured grace window, which is why a printed room code never expires when the schedule moves.
  - `GetAttendanceStatsAsync(eventId, ct)` (`:62-64`) fetches the attendance rollup for one event; organizer only (`CheckInService.cs:71-88`).
- **Why it's built this way**: one interface rather than one per surface keeps the whole check-in vocabulary, and the single `checkins` resource it maps to (`CheckInService.cs:19`), in one reviewable place; the six methods correspond one-for-one to the six endpoints on [`CheckInsController`](#checkinscontroller) ([ADR-071](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/071-barcode-scanning-and-qr-display.html) for the scan surfaces, [ADR-072](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/072-qr-badge-check-in-and-points.html) for the check-in and points model).
- **Where it's used**: implemented by [`CheckInService`](#checkinservice) (`CheckInService.cs:17`), registered scoped (`MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:40`), and injected into four pages: [`CheckInScan`](#checkinscan) (`CheckInScan.razor.cs:21`), [`MyBadge`](#mybadge) (`MyBadge.razor.cs:16`), [`OrganizerAttendance`](#organizerattendance) (`OrganizerAttendance.razor.cs:16`), [`RoomCheckIn`](#roomcheckin) (`RoomCheckIn.razor.cs:24`) and [`SponsorVisit`](#sponsorvisit) (`SponsorVisit.razor.cs:25`). Server-side the whole controller sits behind `[FeatureGate(EngagementFeatures.CheckIn)]` (`CheckInsController.cs:32`) with the two self-service writes gated again individually (`:114`, `:139`), see [`EngagementFeatures`](#engagementfeatures) and [ADR-031](https://ivanball.github.io/docs/adr/031-feature-flag-management.html).
